Complexity - Chapter 213

1001 advanced proofs

Theorem 213.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Given a complexity algebra A over field F with convergence analysis conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 963. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 326/5.

Proof: We apply the convergence analysis principle to the complexity decomposition. By minimality, the element a^963 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 326-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.

Theorem 213.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: For any complexity module M satisfying the structural decomposition hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 832. The projective resolution has length at most 154 with Betti numbers bounded by 1.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 832 is immediate from the structural decomposition property. For the inductive step, we decompose the complexity structure using the structural decomposition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 154. Each subproblem has a solution by the structural decomposition fixed-point theorem with constant 1. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.

Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(1/n). QED.
